  <section id="test-plugins">
<h1>Test Plugins<a class="headerlink" href="#test-plugins" title="Link to this heading"></a></h1>
<p>Bandit supports many different tests to detect various security issues in
python code. These tests are created as plugins and new ones can be created to
extend the functionality offered by bandit today.</p>
<section id="writing-tests">
<h2>Writing Tests<a class="headerlink" href="#writing-tests" title="Link to this heading"></a></h2>
<dl>
<dt>To write a test:</dt><dd><ul class="simple">
<li><p>Identify a vulnerability to build a test for, and create a new file in
examples/ that contains one or more cases of that vulnerability.</p></li>
<li><p>Create a new Python source file to contain your test, you can reference
existing tests for examples.</p></li>
<li><p>Consider the vulnerability you’re testing for, mark the function with one
or more of the appropriate decorators:</p></li>
</ul>
<blockquote>
<div><ul class="simple">
<li><p>&#64;checks(‘Call’)</p></li>
<li><p>&#64;checks(‘Import’, ‘ImportFrom’)</p></li>
<li><p>&#64;checks(‘Str’)</p></li>
</ul>
</div></blockquote>
<ul class="simple">
<li><p>Register your plugin using the <cite>bandit.plugins</cite> entry point, see example.</p></li>
<li><p>The function that you create should take a parameter “context” which is
an instance of the context class you can query for information about the
current element being examined.  You can also get the raw AST node for
more advanced use cases.  Please see the <cite>context.py</cite> file for more.</p></li>
<li><p>Extend your Bandit configuration file as needed to support your new test.</p></li>
<li><p>Execute Bandit against the test file you defined in <cite>examples/</cite> and ensure
that it detects the vulnerability.  Consider variations on how this
vulnerability might present itself and extend the example file and the test
function accordingly.</p></li>
</ul>
</dd>
</dl>
</section>
<section id="config-generation">
<h2>Config Generation<a class="headerlink" href="#config-generation" title="Link to this heading"></a></h2>
<p>In Bandit 1.0+ config files are optional. Plugins that need config settings are
required to implement a module global <cite>gen_config</cite> function. This function is
called with a single parameter, the test plugin name. It should return a
dictionary with keys being the config option names and values being the default
settings for each option. An example <cite>gen_config</cite> might look like the following:</p>
<div class="highlight-python notranslate"><div class="highlight"><pre><span></span><span class="k">def</span><span class="w"> </span><span class="nf">gen_config</span><span class="p">(</span><span class="n">name</span><span class="p">):</span>
    <span class="k">if</span> <span class="n">name</span> <span class="o">==</span> <span class="s1">&#39;try_except_continue&#39;</span><span class="p">:</span>
        <span class="k">return</span> <span class="p">{</span><span class="s1">&#39;check_typed_exception&#39;</span><span class="p">:</span> <span class="kc">False</span><span class="p">}</span>
</pre></div>
</div>
<p>When no config file is specified, or when the chosen file has no section
pertaining to a given plugin, <cite>gen_config</cite> will be called to provide defaults.</p>
<p>The config file generation tool <cite>bandit-config-generator</cite> will also call
<cite>gen_config</cite> on all discovered plugins to produce template config blocks. If
the defaults are acceptable then these blocks may be deleted to create a
minimal configuration, or otherwise edited as needed. The above example would
produce the following config snippet.</p>
<div class="highlight-yaml notranslate"><div class="highlight"><pre><span></span><span class="nt">try_except_continue</span><span class="p">:</span><span class="w"> </span><span class="p p-Indicator">{</span><span class="nt">check_typed_exception</span><span class="p">:</span><span class="w"> </span><span class="nv">false</span><span class="p p-Indicator">}</span>
</pre></div>
</div>
</section>
<section id="example-test-plugin">
<h2>Example Test Plugin<a class="headerlink" href="#example-test-plugin" title="Link to this heading"></a></h2>
<div class="highlight-python notranslate"><div class="highlight"><pre><span></span><span class="nd">@bandit</span><span class="o">.</span><span class="n">checks</span><span class="p">(</span><span class="s1">&#39;Call&#39;</span><span class="p">)</span>
<span class="k">def</span><span class="w"> </span><span class="nf">prohibit_unsafe_deserialization</span><span class="p">(</span><span class="n">context</span><span class="p">):</span>
    <span class="k">if</span> <span class="s1">&#39;unsafe_load&#39;</span> <span class="ow">in</span> <span class="n">context</span><span class="o">.</span><span class="n">call_function_name_qual</span><span class="p">:</span>
        <span class="k">return</span> <span class="n">bandit</span><span class="o">.</span><span class="n">Issue</span><span class="p">(</span>
            <span class="n">severity</span><span class="o">=</span><span class="n">bandit</span><span class="o">.</span><span class="n">HIGH</span><span class="p">,</span>
            <span class="n">confidence</span><span class="o">=</span><span class="n">bandit</span><span class="o">.</span><span class="n">HIGH</span><span class="p">,</span>
            <span class="n">text</span><span class="o">=</span><span class="s2">&quot;Unsafe deserialization detected.&quot;</span>
        <span class="p">)</span>
</pre></div>
</div>
<p>To register your plugin, you have two options:</p>
<ol class="arabic">
<li><p>If you’re using setuptools directly, add something like the following to
your <cite>setup</cite> call:</p>
<div class="highlight-default notranslate"><div class="highlight"><pre><span></span><span class="c1"># If you have an imaginary bson formatter in the bandit_bson module</span>
<span class="c1"># and a function called `formatter`.</span>
<span class="n">entry_points</span><span class="o">=</span><span class="p">{</span><span class="s1">&#39;bandit.formatters&#39;</span><span class="p">:</span> <span class="p">[</span><span class="s1">&#39;bson = bandit_bson:formatter&#39;</span><span class="p">]}</span>
<span class="c1"># Or a check for using mako templates in bandit_mako that</span>
<span class="n">entry_points</span><span class="o">=</span><span class="p">{</span><span class="s1">&#39;bandit.plugins&#39;</span><span class="p">:</span> <span class="p">[</span><span class="s1">&#39;mako = bandit_mako&#39;</span><span class="p">]}</span>
</pre></div>
</div>
</li>
<li><p>If you’re using pbr, add something like the following to your <cite>setup.cfg</cite>
file:</p>
<div class="highlight-default notranslate"><div class="highlight"><pre><span></span><span class="p">[</span><span class="n">entry_points</span><span class="p">]</span>
<span class="n">bandit</span><span class="o">.</span><span class="n">formatters</span> <span class="o">=</span>
    <span class="n">bson</span> <span class="o">=</span> <span class="n">bandit_bson</span><span class="p">:</span><span class="n">formatter</span>
<span class="n">bandit</span><span class="o">.</span><span class="n">plugins</span> <span class="o">=</span>
    <span class="n">mako</span> <span class="o">=</span> <span class="n">bandit_mako</span>
</pre></div>
</div>
</li>
</ol>
</section>

  <section id="b101-assert-used">
<h1>B101: assert_used<a class="headerlink" href="#b101-assert-used" title="Link to this heading"></a></h1>
<section id="b101-test-for-use-of-assert">
<h2>B101: Test for use of assert<a class="headerlink" href="#b101-test-for-use-of-assert" title="Link to this heading"></a></h2>
<p>This plugin test checks for the use of the Python <code class="docutils literal notranslate"><span class="pre">assert</span></code> keyword. It was
discovered that some projects used assert to enforce interface constraints.
However, assert is removed with compiling to optimised byte code (<cite>python -O</cite>
producing *.opt-1.pyc files). This caused various protections to be removed.
Consider raising a semantically meaningful error or <code class="docutils literal notranslate"><span class="pre">AssertionError</span></code> instead.</p>
<p>Please see
<a class="reference external" href="https://docs.python.org/3/reference/simple_stmts.html#the-assert-statement">https://docs.python.org/3/reference/simple_stmts.html#the-assert-statement</a> for
more info on <code class="docutils literal notranslate"><span class="pre">assert</span></code>.</p>
<p><strong>Config Options:</strong></p>
<p>You can configure files that skip this check. This is often useful when you
use assert statements in test cases.</p>
<div class="highlight-yaml notranslate"><div class="highlight"><pre><span></span><span class="nt">assert_used</span><span class="p">:</span>
<span class="w">  </span><span class="nt">skips</span><span class="p">:</span><span class="w"> </span><span class="p p-Indicator">[</span><span class="s">&#39;*_test.py&#39;</span><span class="p p-Indicator">,</span><span class="w"> </span><span class="s">&#39;*test_*.py&#39;</span><span class="p p-Indicator">]</span>
</pre></div>
</div>
<dl class="field-list simple">
<dt class="field-odd">Example<span class="colon">:</span></dt>
<dd class="field-odd"><p></p></dd>
</dl>
<div class="highlight-none notranslate"><div class="highlight"><pre><span></span>&gt;&gt; Issue: Use of assert detected. The enclosed code will be removed when
   compiling to optimised byte code.
   Severity: Low   Confidence: High
   CWE: CWE-703 (https://cwe.mitre.org/data/definitions/703.html)
   Location: ./examples/assert.py:1
1 assert logged_in
2 display_assets()
</pre></div>
</div>
<div class="admonition seealso">
<p class="admonition-title">See also</p>
<ul class="simple">
<li><p><a class="reference external" href="https://bugs.launchpad.net/juniperopenstack/+bug/1456193">https://bugs.launchpad.net/juniperopenstack/+bug/1456193</a></p></li>
<li><p><a class="reference external" href="https://bugs.launchpad.net/heat/+bug/1397883">https://bugs.launchpad.net/heat/+bug/1397883</a></p></li>
<li><p><a class="reference external" href="https://docs.python.org/3/reference/simple_stmts.html#the-assert-statement">https://docs.python.org/3/reference/simple_stmts.html#the-assert-statement</a></p></li>
<li><p><a class="reference external" href="https://cwe.mitre.org/data/definitions/703.html">https://cwe.mitre.org/data/definitions/703.html</a></p></li>
</ul>
</div>
<div class="versionadded">
<p><span class="versionmodified added">Added in version 0.11.0.</span></p>
</div>
<div class="versionchanged">
<p><span class="versionmodified changed">Changed in version 1.7.3: </span>CWE information added</p>
</div>

  <section id="b102-exec-used">
<h1>B102: exec_used<a class="headerlink" href="#b102-exec-used" title="Link to this heading"></a></h1>
<section id="b102-test-for-the-use-of-exec">
<h2>B102: Test for the use of exec<a class="headerlink" href="#b102-test-for-the-use-of-exec" title="Link to this heading"></a></h2>
<p>This plugin test checks for the use of Python’s <cite>exec</cite> method or keyword. The
Python docs succinctly describe why the use of <cite>exec</cite> is risky.</p>
<dl class="field-list simple">
<dt class="field-odd">Example<span class="colon">:</span></dt>
<dd class="field-odd"><p></p></dd>
</dl>
<div class="highlight-none notranslate"><div class="highlight"><pre><span></span>&gt;&gt; Issue: Use of exec detected.
   Severity: Medium   Confidence: High
   CWE: CWE-78 (https://cwe.mitre.org/data/definitions/78.html)
   Location: ./examples/exec.py:2
1 exec(&quot;do evil&quot;)
</pre></div>
</div>
<div class="admonition seealso">
<p class="admonition-title">See also</p>
<ul class="simple">
<li><p><a class="reference external" href="https://docs.python.org/3/library/functions.html#exec">https://docs.python.org/3/library/functions.html#exec</a></p></li>
<li><p><a class="reference external" href="https://www.python.org/dev/peps/pep-0551/#background">https://www.python.org/dev/peps/pep-0551/#background</a></p></li>
<li><p><a class="reference external" href="https://www.python.org/dev/peps/pep-0578/#suggested-audit-hook-locations">https://www.python.org/dev/peps/pep-0578/#suggested-audit-hook-locations</a></p></li>
<li><p><a class="reference external" href="https://cwe.mitre.org/data/definitions/78.html">https://cwe.mitre.org/data/definitions/78.html</a></p></li>
</ul>
</div>
<div class="versionadded">
<p><span class="versionmodified added">Added in version 0.9.0.</span></p>
</div>
<div class="versionchanged">
<p><span class="versionmodified changed">Changed in version 1.7.3: </span>CWE information added</p>
</div>
</section>
</section>

  <section id="b103-set-bad-file-permissions">
<h1>B103: set_bad_file_permissions<a class="headerlink" href="#b103-set-bad-file-permissions" title="Link to this heading"></a></h1>
<section id="b103-test-for-setting-permissive-file-permissions">
<h2>B103: Test for setting permissive file permissions<a class="headerlink" href="#b103-test-for-setting-permissive-file-permissions" title="Link to this heading"></a></h2>
<p>POSIX based operating systems utilize a permissions model to protect access to
parts of the file system. This model supports three roles “owner”, “group”
and “world” each role may have a combination of “read”, “write” or “execute”
flags sets. Python provides <code class="docutils literal notranslate"><span class="pre">chmod</span></code> to manipulate POSIX style permissions.</p>
<p>This plugin test looks for the use of <code class="docutils literal notranslate"><span class="pre">chmod</span></code> and will alert when it is used
to set particularly permissive control flags. A MEDIUM warning is generated if
a file is set to group write or executable and a HIGH warning is reported if a
file is set world write or executable. Warnings are given with HIGH confidence.</p>
<dl class="field-list simple">
<dt class="field-odd">Example<span class="colon">:</span></dt>
<dd class="field-odd"><p></p></dd>
</dl>
<div class="highlight-none notranslate"><div class="highlight"><pre><span></span>&gt;&gt; Issue: Probable insecure usage of temp file/directory.
   Severity: Medium   Confidence: Medium
   CWE: CWE-732 (https://cwe.mitre.org/data/definitions/732.html)
   Location: ./examples/os-chmod.py:15
14  os.chmod(&#39;/etc/hosts&#39;, 0o777)
15  os.chmod(&#39;/tmp/oh_hai&#39;, 0x1ff)
16  os.chmod(&#39;/etc/passwd&#39;, stat.S_IRWXU)

&gt;&gt; Issue: Chmod setting a permissive mask 0777 on file (key_file).
   Severity: High   Confidence: High
   CWE: CWE-732 (https://cwe.mitre.org/data/definitions/732.html)
   Location: ./examples/os-chmod.py:17
16  os.chmod(&#39;/etc/passwd&#39;, stat.S_IRWXU)
17  os.chmod(key_file, 0o777)
18
</pre></div>
</div>
<div class="admonition seealso">
<p class="admonition-title">See also</p>
<ul class="simple">
<li><p><a class="reference external" href="https://security.openstack.org/guidelines/dg_apply-restrictive-file-permissions.html">https://security.openstack.org/guidelines/dg_apply-restrictive-file-permissions.html</a></p></li>
<li><p><a class="reference external" href="https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/File_system_permissions">https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/File_system_permissions</a></p></li>
<li><p><a class="reference external" href="https://security.openstack.org">https://security.openstack.org</a></p></li>
<li><p><a class="reference external" href="https://cwe.mitre.org/data/definitions/732.html">https://cwe.mitre.org/data/definitions/732.html</a></p></li>
</ul>
</div>
<div class="versionadded">
<p><span class="versionmodified added">Added in version 0.9.0.</span></p>
</div>
<div class="versionchanged">

</div>
<div class="versionchanged">

</div>

  <section id="b104-hardcoded-bind-all-interfaces">
<h1>B104: hardcoded_bind_all_interfaces<a class="headerlink" href="#b104-hardcoded-bind-all-interfaces" title="Link to this heading"></a></h1>
<section id="b104-test-for-binding-to-all-interfaces">
<h2>B104: Test for binding to all interfaces<a class="headerlink" href="#b104-test-for-binding-to-all-interfaces" title="Link to this heading"></a></h2>
<p>Binding to all network interfaces can potentially open up a service to traffic
on unintended interfaces, that may not be properly documented or secured. This
plugin test looks for a string pattern “0.0.0.0” that may indicate a hardcoded
binding to all network interfaces.</p>
<dl class="field-list simple">
<dt class="field-odd">Example<span class="colon">:</span></dt>
<dd class="field-odd"><p></p></dd>
</dl>
<div class="highlight-none notranslate"><div class="highlight"><pre><span></span>&gt;&gt; Issue: Possible binding to all interfaces.
   Severity: Medium   Confidence: Medium
   CWE: CWE-605 (https://cwe.mitre.org/data/definitions/605.html)
   Location: ./examples/binding.py:4
3   s = socket.socket(socket.AF_INET, socket.SOCK_STREAM)
4   s.bind((&#39;0.0.0.0&#39;, 31137))
5   s.bind((&#39;192.168.0.1&#39;, 8080))
</pre></div>
</div>
<div class="admonition seealso">
<p class="admonition-title">See also</p>
<ul class="simple">
<li><p><a class="reference external" href="https://nvd.nist.gov/vuln/detail/CVE-2018-1281">https://nvd.nist.gov/vuln/detail/CVE-2018-1281</a></p></li>
<li><p><a class="reference external" href="https://cwe.mitre.org/data/definitions/605.html">https://cwe.mitre.org/data/definitions/605.html</a></p></li>
</ul>
</div>
<div class="versionadded">
<p><span class="versionmodified added">Added in version 0.9.0.</span></p>
</div>
<div class="versionchanged">
<p><span class="versionmodified changed">Changed in version 1.7.3: </span>CWE information added</p>
</div>
</section>
</section>

           <div itemprop="articleBody">
             
  <section id="b105-hardcoded-password-string">
<h1>B105: hardcoded_password_string<a class="headerlink" href="#b105-hardcoded-password-string" title="Link to this heading"></a></h1>
<dl class="py function">
<dt class="sig sig-object py">
<span class="sig-prename descclassname"><span class="pre">bandit.plugins.general_hardcoded_password.</span></span><span class="sig-name descname"><span class="pre">hardcoded_password_string</span></span><span class="sig-paren">(</span><em class="sig-param"><span class="n"><span class="pre">context</span></span></em><span class="sig-paren">)</span><a class="reference internal" href="../_modules/bandit/plugins/general_hardcoded_password.html#hardcoded_password_string"><span class="viewcode-link"><span class="pre">[source]</span></span></a></dt>
<dd><p><strong>B105: Test for use of hard-coded password strings</strong></p>
<p>The use of hard-coded passwords increases the possibility of password
guessing tremendously. This plugin test looks for all string literals and
checks the following conditions:</p>
<ul class="simple">
<li><p>assigned to a variable that looks like a password</p></li>
<li><p>assigned to a dict key that looks like a password</p></li>
<li><p>assigned to a class attribute that looks like a password</p></li>
<li><p>used in a comparison with a variable that looks like a password</p></li>
</ul>
<p>Variables are considered to look like a password if they have match any one
of:</p>
<ul class="simple">
<li><p>“password”</p></li>
<li><p>“pass”</p></li>
<li><p>“passwd”</p></li>
<li><p>“pwd”</p></li>
<li><p>“secret”</p></li>
<li><p>“token”</p></li>
<li><p>“secrete”</p></li>
</ul>
<p>Note: this can be noisy and may generate false positives.</p>
<p><strong>Config Options:</strong></p>
<p>None</p>
<dl class="field-list simple">
<dt class="field-odd">Example<span class="colon">:</span></dt>
<dd class="field-odd"><p></p></dd>
</dl>
<div class="highlight-none notranslate"><div class="highlight"><pre><span></span>&gt;&gt; Issue: Possible hardcoded password &#39;(root)&#39;
   Severity: Low   Confidence: Low
   CWE: CWE-259 (https://cwe.mitre.org/data/definitions/259.html)
   Location: ./examples/hardcoded-passwords.py:5
4 def someFunction2(password):
5     if password == &quot;root&quot;:
6         print(&quot;OK, logged in&quot;)
</pre></div>
</div>
<div class="admonition seealso">
<p class="admonition-title">See also</p>
<ul class="simple">
<li><p><a class="reference external" href="https://www.owasp.org/index.php/Use_of_hard-coded_password">https://www.owasp.org/index.php/Use_of_hard-coded_password</a></p></li>
<li><p><a class="reference external" href="https://cwe.mitre.org/data/definitions/259.html">https://cwe.mitre.org/data/definitions/259.html</a></p></li>
</ul>
</div>
<div class="versionadded">
<p><span class="versionmodified added">Added in version 0.9.0.</span></p>
</div>
<div class="versionchanged">
<p><span class="versionmodified changed">Changed in version 1.7.3: </span>CWE information added</p>
</div>
</dd></dl>

</section>

          <div role="main" class="document" itemscope="itemscope" itemtype="http://schema.org/Article">
           <div itemprop="articleBody">
             
  <section id="b106-hardcoded-password-funcarg">
<h1>B106: hardcoded_password_funcarg<a class="headerlink" href="#b106-hardcoded-password-funcarg" title="Link to this heading"></a></h1>
<dl class="py function">
<dt class="sig sig-object py">
<span class="sig-prename descclassname"><span class="pre">bandit.plugins.general_hardcoded_password.</span></span><span class="sig-name descname"><span class="pre">hardcoded_password_funcarg</span></span><span class="sig-paren">(</span><em class="sig-param"><span class="n"><span class="pre">context</span></span></em><span class="sig-paren">)</span><a class="reference internal" href="../_modules/bandit/plugins/general_hardcoded_password.html#hardcoded_password_funcarg"><span class="viewcode-link"><span class="pre">[source]</span></span></a></dt>
<dd><p><strong>B106: Test for use of hard-coded password function arguments</strong></p>
<p>The use of hard-coded passwords increases the possibility of password
guessing tremendously. This plugin test looks for all function calls being
passed a keyword argument that is a string literal. It checks that the
assigned local variable does not look like a password.</p>
<p>Variables are considered to look like a password if they have match any one
of:</p>
<ul class="simple">
<li><p>“password”</p></li>
<li><p>“pass”</p></li>
<li><p>“passwd”</p></li>
<li><p>“pwd”</p></li>
<li><p>“secret”</p></li>
<li><p>“token”</p></li>
<li><p>“secrete”</p></li>
</ul>
<p>Note: this can be noisy and may generate false positives.</p>
<p><strong>Config Options:</strong></p>
<p>None</p>
<dl class="field-list simple">
<dt class="field-odd">Example<span class="colon">:</span></dt>
<dd class="field-odd"><p></p></dd>
</dl>
<div class="highlight-none notranslate"><div class="highlight"><pre><span></span>&gt;&gt; Issue: [B106:hardcoded_password_funcarg] Possible hardcoded
password: &#39;blerg&#39;
   Severity: Low   Confidence: Medium
   CWE: CWE-259 (https://cwe.mitre.org/data/definitions/259.html)
   Location: ./examples/hardcoded-passwords.py:16
15
16    doLogin(password=&quot;blerg&quot;)
</pre></div>
</div>
<div class="admonition seealso">
<p class="admonition-title">See also</p>
<ul class="simple">
<li><p><a class="reference external" href="https://www.owasp.org/index.php/Use_of_hard-coded_password">https://www.owasp.org/index.php/Use_of_hard-coded_password</a></p></li>
<li><p><a class="reference external" href="https://cwe.mitre.org/data/definitions/259.html">https://cwe.mitre.org/data/definitions/259.html</a></p></li>
</ul>
</div>
<div class="versionadded">
<p><span class="versionmodified added">Added in version 0.9.0.</span></p>
</div>
<div class="versionchanged">
<p><span class="versionmodified changed">Changed in version 1.7.3: </span>CWE information added</p>
</div>
</dd></dl>

</section>

          <div role="main" class="document" itemscope="itemscope" itemtype="http://schema.org/Article">
           <div itemprop="articleBody">
             
  <section id="b107-hardcoded-password-default">
<h1>B107: hardcoded_password_default<a class="headerlink" href="#b107-hardcoded-password-default" title="Link to this heading"></a></h1>
<dl class="py function">
<dt class="sig sig-object py">
<span class="sig-prename descclassname"><span class="pre">bandit.plugins.general_hardcoded_password.</span></span><span class="sig-name descname"><span class="pre">hardcoded_password_default</span></span><span class="sig-paren">(</span><em class="sig-param"><span class="n"><span class="pre">context</span></span></em><span class="sig-paren">)</span><a class="reference internal" href="../_modules/bandit/plugins/general_hardcoded_password.html#hardcoded_password_default"><span class="viewcode-link"><span class="pre">[source]</span></span></a></dt>
<dd><p><strong>B107: Test for use of hard-coded password argument defaults</strong></p>
<p>The use of hard-coded passwords increases the possibility of password
guessing tremendously. This plugin test looks for all function definitions
that specify a default string literal for some argument. It checks that
the argument does not look like a password.</p>
<p>Variables are considered to look like a password if they have match any one
of:</p>
<ul class="simple">
<li><p>“password”</p></li>
<li><p>“pass”</p></li>
<li><p>“passwd”</p></li>
<li><p>“pwd”</p></li>
<li><p>“secret”</p></li>
<li><p>“token”</p></li>
<li><p>“secrete”</p></li>
</ul>
<p>Note: this can be noisy and may generate false positives.  We do not
report on None values which can be legitimately used as a default value,
when initializing a function or class.</p>
<p><strong>Config Options:</strong></p>
<p>None</p>
<dl class="field-list simple">
<dt class="field-odd">Example<span class="colon">:</span></dt>
<dd class="field-odd"><p></p></dd>
</dl>
<div class="highlight-none notranslate"><div class="highlight"><pre><span></span>&gt;&gt; Issue: [B107:hardcoded_password_default] Possible hardcoded
password: &#39;Admin&#39;
   Severity: Low   Confidence: Medium
   CWE: CWE-259 (https://cwe.mitre.org/data/definitions/259.html)
   Location: ./examples/hardcoded-passwords.py:1

1    def someFunction(user, password=&quot;Admin&quot;):
2      print(&quot;Hi &quot; + user)
</pre></div>
</div>
<div class="admonition seealso">
<p class="admonition-title">See also</p>
<ul class="simple">
<li><p><a class="reference external" href="https://www.owasp.org/index.php/Use_of_hard-coded_password">https://www.owasp.org/index.php/Use_of_hard-coded_password</a></p></li>
<li><p><a class="reference external" href="https://cwe.mitre.org/data/definitions/259.html">https://cwe.mitre.org/data/definitions/259.html</a></p></li>
</ul>
</div>
<div class="versionadded">
<p><span class="versionmodified added">Added in version 0.9.0.</span></p>
</div>
<div class="versionchanged">
<p><span class="versionmodified changed">Changed in version 1.7.3: </span>CWE information added</p>
</div>
</dd></dl>

  <section id="b108-hardcoded-tmp-directory">
<h1>B108: hardcoded_tmp_directory<a class="headerlink" href="#b108-hardcoded-tmp-directory" title="Link to this heading"></a></h1>
<section id="b108-test-for-insecure-usage-of-tmp-file-directory">
<h2>B108: Test for insecure usage of tmp file/directory<a class="headerlink" href="#b108-test-for-insecure-usage-of-tmp-file-directory" title="Link to this heading"></a></h2>
<p>Safely creating a temporary file or directory means following a number of rules
(see the references for more details). This plugin test looks for strings
starting with (configurable) commonly used temporary paths, for example:</p>
<blockquote>
<div><ul class="simple">
<li><p>/tmp</p></li>
<li><p>/var/tmp</p></li>
<li><p>/dev/shm</p></li>
</ul>
</div></blockquote>
<p><strong>Config Options:</strong></p>
<p>This test plugin takes a similarly named config block,
<cite>hardcoded_tmp_directory</cite>. The config block provides a Python list, <cite>tmp_dirs</cite>,
that lists string fragments indicating possible temporary file paths. Any
string starting with one of these fragments will report a MEDIUM confidence
issue.</p>
<div class="highlight-yaml notranslate"><div class="highlight"><pre><span></span><span class="nt">hardcoded_tmp_directory</span><span class="p">:</span>
<span class="w">    </span><span class="nt">tmp_dirs</span><span class="p">:</span><span class="w"> </span><span class="p p-Indicator">[</span><span class="s">&#39;/tmp&#39;</span><span class="p p-Indicator">,</span><span class="w"> </span><span class="s">&#39;/var/tmp&#39;</span><span class="p p-Indicator">,</span><span class="w"> </span><span class="s">&#39;/dev/shm&#39;</span><span class="p p-Indicator">]</span>
</pre></div>
</div>
<dl class="field-list simple">
<dt class="field-odd">Example<span class="colon">:</span></dt>
<dd class="field-odd"><p></p></dd>
</dl>
<div class="admonition seealso">
<p class="admonition-title">See also</p>
<ul class="simple">
<li><p><a class="reference external" href="https://security.openstack.org/guidelines/dg_using-temporary-files-securely.html">https://security.openstack.org/guidelines/dg_using-temporary-files-securely.html</a></p></li>
<li><p><a class="reference external" href="https://cwe.mitre.org/data/definitions/377.html">https://cwe.mitre.org/data/definitions/377.html</a></p></li>
</ul>
</div>
<div class="versionadded">
<p><span class="versionmodified added">Added in version 0.9.0.</span></p>
</div>
<div class="versionchanged">
<p><span class="versionmodified changed">Changed in version 1.7.3: </span>CWE information added</p>
</div>

  <section id="b109-password-config-option-not-marked-secret">
<h1>B109: password_config_option_not_marked_secret<a class="headerlink" href="#b109-password-config-option-not-marked-secret" title="Link to this heading"></a></h1>
<p>This plugin has been removed.</p>
<p>B109: Test for a password based config option not marked secret</p>
<p>Passwords are sensitive and must be protected appropriately. In OpenStack
Oslo there is an option to mark options “secret” which will ensure that they
are not logged. This plugin detects usages of oslo configuration functions
that appear to deal with strings ending in ‘password’ and flag usages where
they have not been marked secret.</p>
<p>If such a value is found a MEDIUM severity error is generated. If ‘False’ or
‘None’ are explicitly set, Bandit will return a MEDIUM confidence issue. If
Bandit can’t determine the value of secret it will return a LOW confidence
issue.</p>
<p><strong>Config Options:</strong></p>
<div class="highlight-yaml notranslate"><div class="highlight"><pre><span></span><span class="nt">password_config_option_not_marked_secret</span><span class="p">:</span>
<span class="w">    </span><span class="nt">function_names</span><span class="p">:</span>
<span class="w">        </span><span class="p p-Indicator">-</span><span class="w"> </span><span class="l l-Scalar l-Scalar-Plain">oslo.config.cfg.StrOpt</span>
<span class="w">        </span><span class="p p-Indicator">-</span><span class="w"> </span><span class="l l-Scalar l-Scalar-Plain">oslo_config.cfg.StrOpt</span>
</pre></div>
</div>
<dl class="field-list simple">
<dt class="field-odd">Example<span class="colon">:</span></dt>
<dd class="field-odd"><p></p></dd>
</dl>
<div class="highlight-none notranslate"><div class="highlight"><pre><span></span>&gt;&gt; Issue: [password_config_option_not_marked_secret] oslo config option
possibly not marked secret=True identified.
   Severity: Medium   Confidence: Low
   Location: examples/secret-config-option.py:12
11                  help=&quot;User&#39;s password&quot;),
12       cfg.StrOpt(&#39;nova_password&#39;,
13                  secret=secret,
14                  help=&quot;Nova user password&quot;),
15   ]

&gt;&gt; Issue: [password_config_option_not_marked_secret] oslo config option not
marked secret=True identified, security issue.
   Severity: Medium   Confidence: Medium
   Location: examples/secret-config-option.py:21
20                  help=&quot;LDAP ubind ser name&quot;),
21       cfg.StrOpt(&#39;ldap_password&#39;,
22                  help=&quot;LDAP bind user password&quot;),
23       cfg.StrOpt(&#39;ldap_password_attribute&#39;,
</pre></div>
</div>
<div class="admonition seealso">
<p class="admonition-title">See also</p>
<ul class="simple">
<li><p><a class="reference external" href="https://security.openstack.org/guidelines/dg_protect-sensitive-data-in-files.html">https://security.openstack.org/guidelines/dg_protect-sensitive-data-in-files.html</a></p></li>
</ul>
</div>
<div class="versionadded">
<p><span class="versionmodified added">Added in version 0.10.0.</span></p>
</div>
<div class="deprecated">
<p><span class="versionmodified deprecated">Deprecated since version 1.5.0: </span>This plugin was removed</p>
</div>
</section>

  <section id="b110-try-except-pass">
<h1>B110: try_except_pass<a class="headerlink" href="#b110-try-except-pass" title="Link to this heading"></a></h1>
<section id="b110-test-for-a-pass-in-the-except-block">
<h2>B110: Test for a pass in the except block<a class="headerlink" href="#b110-test-for-a-pass-in-the-except-block" title="Link to this heading"></a></h2>
<p>Errors in Python code bases are typically communicated using <code class="docutils literal notranslate"><span class="pre">Exceptions</span></code>.
An exception object is ‘raised’ in the event of an error and can be ‘caught’ at
a later point in the program, typically some error handling or logging action
will then be performed.</p>
<p>However, it is possible to catch an exception and silently ignore it. This is
illustrated with the following example</p>
<div class="highlight-python notranslate"><div class="highlight"><pre><span></span><span class="k">try</span><span class="p">:</span>
  <span class="n">do_some_stuff</span><span class="p">()</span>
<span class="k">except</span> <span class="ne">Exception</span><span class="p">:</span>
  <span class="k">pass</span>
</pre></div>
</div>
<p>This pattern is considered bad practice in general, but also represents a
potential security issue. A larger than normal volume of errors from a service
can indicate an attempt is being made to disrupt or interfere with it. Thus
errors should, at the very least, be logged.</p>
<p>There are rare situations where it is desirable to suppress errors, but this is
typically done with specific exception types, rather than the base Exception
class (or no type). To accommodate this, the test may be configured to ignore
‘try, except, pass’ where the exception is typed. For example, the following
would not generate a warning if the configuration option
<code class="docutils literal notranslate"><span class="pre">checked_typed_exception</span></code> is set to False:</p>
<div class="highlight-python notranslate"><div class="highlight"><pre><span></span><span class="k">try</span><span class="p">:</span>
  <span class="n">do_some_stuff</span><span class="p">()</span>
<span class="k">except</span> <span class="ne">ZeroDivisionError</span><span class="p">:</span>
  <span class="k">pass</span>
</pre></div>
</div>
<p><strong>Config Options:</strong></p>
<div class="highlight-yaml notranslate"><div class="highlight"><pre><span></span><span class="nt">try_except_pass</span><span class="p">:</span>
<span class="w">  </span><span class="nt">check_typed_exception</span><span class="p">:</span><span class="w"> </span><span class="l l-Scalar l-Scalar-Plain">True</span>
</pre></div>
</div>
<dl class="field-list simple">
<dt class="field-odd">Example<span class="colon">:</span></dt>
<dd class="field-odd"><p></p></dd>
</dl>
<div class="highlight-none notranslate"><div class="highlight"><pre><span></span>&gt;&gt; Issue: Try, Except, Pass detected.
   Severity: Low   Confidence: High
   CWE: CWE-703 (https://cwe.mitre.org/data/definitions/703.html)
   Location: ./examples/try_except_pass.py:4
3        a = 1
4    except:
5        pass
</pre></div>
</div>
<div class="admonition seealso">
<p class="admonition-title">See also</p>
<ul class="simple">
<li><p><a class="reference external" href="https://security.openstack.org">https://security.openstack.org</a></p></li>
<li><p><a class="reference external" href="https://cwe.mitre.org/data/definitions/703.html">https://cwe.mitre.org/data/definitions/703.html</a></p></li>
</ul>
</div>
<div class="versionadded">
<p><span class="versionmodified added">Added in version 0.13.0.</span></p>
</div>
<div class="versionchanged">
<p><span class="versionmodified changed">Changed in version 1.7.3: </span>CWE information added</p>
</div>

  <section id="b111-execute-with-run-as-root-equals-true">
<h1>B111: execute_with_run_as_root_equals_true<a class="headerlink" href="#b111-execute-with-run-as-root-equals-true" title="Link to this heading"></a></h1>
<p>This plugin has been removed.</p>
<p>B111: Test for the use of rootwrap running as root</p>
<p>Running commands as root dramatically increase their potential risk. Running
commands with restricted user privileges provides defense in depth against
command injection attacks, or developer and configuration error. This plugin
test checks for specific methods being called with a keyword parameter
<cite>run_as_root</cite> set to True, a common OpenStack idiom.</p>
<p><strong>Config Options:</strong></p>
<p>This test plugin takes a similarly named configuration block,
<cite>execute_with_run_as_root_equals_true</cite>, providing a list, <cite>function_names</cite>, of
function names. A call to any of these named functions will be checked for a
<cite>run_as_root</cite> keyword parameter, and if True, will report a Low severity
issue.</p>
<div class="highlight-yaml notranslate"><div class="highlight"><pre><span></span><span class="nt">execute_with_run_as_root_equals_true</span><span class="p">:</span>
<span class="w">    </span><span class="nt">function_names</span><span class="p">:</span>
<span class="w">        </span><span class="p p-Indicator">-</span><span class="w"> </span><span class="l l-Scalar l-Scalar-Plain">ceilometer.utils.execute</span>
<span class="w">        </span><span class="p p-Indicator">-</span><span class="w"> </span><span class="l l-Scalar l-Scalar-Plain">cinder.utils.execute</span>
<span class="w">        </span><span class="p p-Indicator">-</span><span class="w"> </span><span class="l l-Scalar l-Scalar-Plain">neutron.agent.linux.utils.execute</span>
<span class="w">        </span><span class="p p-Indicator">-</span><span class="w"> </span><span class="l l-Scalar l-Scalar-Plain">nova.utils.execute</span>
<span class="w">        </span><span class="p p-Indicator">-</span><span class="w"> </span><span class="l l-Scalar l-Scalar-Plain">nova.utils.trycmd</span>
</pre></div>
</div>
<dl class="field-list simple">
<dt class="field-odd">Example<span class="colon">:</span></dt>
<dd class="field-odd"><p></p></dd>
</dl>
<div class="highlight-none notranslate"><div class="highlight"><pre><span></span>&gt;&gt; Issue: Execute with run_as_root=True identified, possible security
   issue.
   Severity: Low   Confidence: Medium
   Location: ./examples/exec-as-root.py:26
25  nova_utils.trycmd(&#39;gcc --version&#39;)
26  nova_utils.trycmd(&#39;gcc --version&#39;, run_as_root=True)
27
</pre></div>
</div>
<div class="admonition seealso">
<p class="admonition-title">See also</p>
<ul class="simple">
<li><p><a class="reference external" href="https://security.openstack.org/guidelines/dg_rootwrap-recommendations-and-plans.html">https://security.openstack.org/guidelines/dg_rootwrap-recommendations-and-plans.html</a></p></li>
<li><p><a class="reference external" href="https://security.openstack.org/guidelines/dg_use-oslo-rootwrap-securely.html">https://security.openstack.org/guidelines/dg_use-oslo-rootwrap-securely.html</a></p></li>
</ul>
</div>
<div class="versionadded">
<p><span class="versionmodified added">Added in version 0.10.0.</span></p>
</div>
<div class="deprecated">
<p><span class="versionmodified deprecated">Deprecated since version 1.5.0: </span>This plugin was removed</p>
</div>

  <section id="b112-try-except-continue">
<h1>B112: try_except_continue<a class="headerlink" href="#b112-try-except-continue" title="Link to this heading"></a></h1>
<section id="b112-test-for-a-continue-in-the-except-block">
<h2>B112: Test for a continue in the except block<a class="headerlink" href="#b112-test-for-a-continue-in-the-except-block" title="Link to this heading"></a></h2>
<p>Errors in Python code bases are typically communicated using <code class="docutils literal notranslate"><span class="pre">Exceptions</span></code>.
An exception object is ‘raised’ in the event of an error and can be ‘caught’ at
a later point in the program, typically some error handling or logging action
will then be performed.</p>
<p>However, it is possible to catch an exception and silently ignore it while in
a loop. This is illustrated with the following example</p>
<div class="highlight-python notranslate"><div class="highlight"><pre><span></span><span class="k">while</span> <span class="n">keep_going</span><span class="p">:</span>
  <span class="k">try</span><span class="p">:</span>
    <span class="n">do_some_stuff</span><span class="p">()</span>
  <span class="k">except</span> <span class="ne">Exception</span><span class="p">:</span>
    <span class="k">continue</span>
</pre></div>
</div>
<p>This pattern is considered bad practice in general, but also represents a
potential security issue. A larger than normal volume of errors from a service
can indicate an attempt is being made to disrupt or interfere with it. Thus
errors should, at the very least, be logged.</p>
<p>There are rare situations where it is desirable to suppress errors, but this is
typically done with specific exception types, rather than the base Exception
class (or no type). To accommodate this, the test may be configured to ignore
‘try, except, continue’ where the exception is typed. For example, the
following would not generate a warning if the configuration option
<code class="docutils literal notranslate"><span class="pre">checked_typed_exception</span></code> is set to False:</p>
<div class="highlight-python notranslate"><div class="highlight"><pre><span></span><span class="k">while</span> <span class="n">keep_going</span><span class="p">:</span>
  <span class="k">try</span><span class="p">:</span>
    <span class="n">do_some_stuff</span><span class="p">()</span>
  <span class="k">except</span> <span class="ne">ZeroDivisionError</span><span class="p">:</span>
    <span class="k">continue</span>
</pre></div>
</div>
<p><strong>Config Options:</strong></p>
<div class="highlight-yaml notranslate"><div class="highlight"><pre><span></span><span class="nt">try_except_continue</span><span class="p">:</span>
<span class="w">  </span><span class="nt">check_typed_exception</span><span class="p">:</span><span class="w"> </span><span class="l l-Scalar l-Scalar-Plain">True</span>
</pre></div>
</div>
<dl class="field-list simple">
<dt class="field-odd">Example<span class="colon">:</span></dt>
<dd class="field-odd"><p></p></dd>
</dl>
<div class="highlight-none notranslate"><div class="highlight"><pre><span></span>&gt;&gt; Issue: Try, Except, Continue detected.
   Severity: Low   Confidence: High
   CWE: CWE-703 (https://cwe.mitre.org/data/definitions/703.html)
   Location: ./examples/try_except_continue.py:5
4            a = i
5        except:
6            continue
</pre></div>
</div>
<div class="admonition seealso">
<p class="admonition-title">See also</p>
<ul class="simple">
<li><p><a class="reference external" href="https://security.openstack.org">https://security.openstack.org</a></p></li>
<li><p><a class="reference external" href="https://cwe.mitre.org/data/definitions/703.html">https://cwe.mitre.org/data/definitions/703.html</a></p></li>
</ul>
</div>
<div class="versionadded">
<p><span class="versionmodified added">Added in version 1.0.0.</span></p>
</div>

</div>

          <div role="main" class="document" itemscope="itemscope" itemtype="http://schema.org/Article">
           <div itemprop="articleBody">
             
  <section id="b113-request-without-timeout">
<h1>B113: request_without_timeout<a class="headerlink" href="#b113-request-without-timeout" title="Link to this heading"></a></h1>
<section id="b113-test-for-missing-requests-timeout">
<h2>B113: Test for missing requests timeout<a class="headerlink" href="#b113-test-for-missing-requests-timeout" title="Link to this heading"></a></h2>
<p>This plugin test checks for <code class="docutils literal notranslate"><span class="pre">requests</span></code> or <code class="docutils literal notranslate"><span class="pre">httpx</span></code> calls without a timeout
specified.</p>
<p>Nearly all production code should use this parameter in nearly all requests,
Failure to do so can cause your program to hang indefinitely.</p>
<p>When request methods are used without the timeout parameter set,
Bandit will return a MEDIUM severity error.</p>
<dl class="field-list simple">
<dt class="field-odd">Example<span class="colon">:</span></dt>
<dd class="field-odd"><p></p></dd>
</dl>
<div class="highlight-none notranslate"><div class="highlight"><pre><span></span>&gt;&gt; Issue: [B113:request_without_timeout] Call to requests without timeout
   Severity: Medium   Confidence: Low
   CWE: CWE-400 (https://cwe.mitre.org/data/definitions/400.html)
   More Info: https://bandit.readthedocs.io/en/latest/plugins/b113_request_without_timeout.html
   Location: examples/requests-missing-timeout.py:3:0
2
3   requests.get(&#39;https://gmail.com&#39;)
4   requests.get(&#39;https://gmail.com&#39;, timeout=None)

--------------------------------------------------
&gt;&gt; Issue: [B113:request_without_timeout] Call to requests with timeout set to None
   Severity: Medium   Confidence: Low
   CWE: CWE-400 (https://cwe.mitre.org/data/definitions/400.html)
   More Info: https://bandit.readthedocs.io/en/latest/plugins/b113_request_without_timeout.html
   Location: examples/requests-missing-timeout.py:4:0
3   requests.get(&#39;https://gmail.com&#39;)
4   requests.get(&#39;https://gmail.com&#39;, timeout=None)
5   requests.get(&#39;https://gmail.com&#39;, timeout=5)
</pre></div>
</div>
<div class="admonition seealso">
<p class="admonition-title">See also</p>
<ul class="simple">
<li><p><a class="reference external" href="https://requests.readthedocs.io/en/latest/user/advanced/#timeouts">https://requests.readthedocs.io/en/latest/user/advanced/#timeouts</a></p></li>
</ul>
</div>
<div class="versionadded">
<p><span class="versionmodified added">Added in version 1.7.5.</span></p>
</div>
<div class="versionchanged">
<p><span class="versionmodified changed">Changed in version 1.7.10: </span>Added check for httpx module</p>
</div>
</section>
</section>

  <section id="b201-flask-debug-true">
<h1>B201: flask_debug_true<a class="headerlink" href="#b201-flask-debug-true" title="Link to this heading"></a></h1>
<section id="b201-test-for-use-of-flask-app-with-debug-set-to-true">
<h2>B201: Test for use of flask app with debug set to true<a class="headerlink" href="#b201-test-for-use-of-flask-app-with-debug-set-to-true" title="Link to this heading"></a></h2>
<p>Running Flask applications in debug mode results in the Werkzeug debugger
being enabled. This includes a feature that allows arbitrary code execution.
Documentation for both Flask <a class="footnote-reference brackets" href="#id4" id="id1" role="doc-noteref"><span class="fn-bracket">[</span>1<span class="fn-bracket">]</span></a> and Werkzeug <a class="footnote-reference brackets" href="#id5" id="id2" role="doc-noteref"><span class="fn-bracket">[</span>2<span class="fn-bracket">]</span></a> strongly suggests that
debug mode should never be enabled on production systems.</p>
<p>Operating a production server with debug mode enabled was the probable cause
of the Patreon breach in 2015 <a class="footnote-reference brackets" href="#id6" id="id3" role="doc-noteref"><span class="fn-bracket">[</span>3<span class="fn-bracket">]</span></a>.</p>
<dl class="field-list simple">
<dt class="field-odd">Example<span class="colon">:</span></dt>
<dd class="field-odd"><p></p></dd>
</dl>
<div class="highlight-none notranslate"><div class="highlight"><pre><span></span>&gt;&gt; Issue: A Flask app appears to be run with debug=True, which exposes
the Werkzeug debugger and allows the execution of arbitrary code.
   Severity: High   Confidence: High
   CWE: CWE-94 (https://cwe.mitre.org/data/definitions/94.html)
   Location: examples/flask_debug.py:10
9 #bad
10    app.run(debug=True)
11
</pre></div>
</div>
<div class="admonition seealso">
<p class="admonition-title">See also</p>
<aside class="footnote-list brackets">
<aside class="footnote brackets" id="id4" role="doc-footnote">
<span class="label"><span class="fn-bracket">[</span><a role="doc-backlink" href="#id1">1</a><span class="fn-bracket">]</span></span>
<p><a class="reference external" href="https://flask.palletsprojects.com/en/1.1.x/quickstart/#debug-mode">https://flask.palletsprojects.com/en/1.1.x/quickstart/#debug-mode</a></p>
</aside>
<aside class="footnote brackets" id="id5" role="doc-footnote">
<span class="label"><span class="fn-bracket">[</span><a role="doc-backlink" href="#id2">2</a><span class="fn-bracket">]</span></span>
<p><a class="reference external" href="https://werkzeug.palletsprojects.com/en/1.0.x/debug/">https://werkzeug.palletsprojects.com/en/1.0.x/debug/</a></p>
</aside>
<aside class="footnote brackets" id="id6" role="doc-footnote">
<span class="label"><span class="fn-bracket">[</span><a role="doc-backlink" href="#id3">3</a><span class="fn-bracket">]</span></span>
<p><a class="reference external" href="https://labs.detectify.com/2015/10/02/how-patreon-got-hacked-publicly-exposed-werkzeug-debugger/">https://labs.detectify.com/2015/10/02/how-patreon-got-hacked-publicly-exposed-werkzeug-debugger/</a></p>
</aside>
</aside>
</div>
<div class="versionadded">
<p><span class="versionmodified added">Added in version 0.15.0.</span></p>
</div>
<div class="versionchanged">
<p><span class="versionmodified changed">Changed in version 1.7.3: </span>CWE information added</p>
</div>

  <section id="b202-tarfile-unsafe-members">
<h1>B202: tarfile_unsafe_members<a class="headerlink" href="#b202-tarfile-unsafe-members" title="Link to this heading"></a></h1>
<section id="b202-test-for-tarfile-extractall">
<h2>B202: Test for tarfile.extractall<a class="headerlink" href="#b202-test-for-tarfile-extractall" title="Link to this heading"></a></h2>
<p>This plugin will look for usage of <code class="docutils literal notranslate"><span class="pre">tarfile.extractall()</span></code></p>
<p>Severity are set as follows:</p>
<ul class="simple">
<li><p><code class="docutils literal notranslate"><span class="pre">tarfile.extractall(members=function(tarfile))</span></code> - LOW</p></li>
<li><p><code class="docutils literal notranslate"><span class="pre">tarfile.extractall(members=?)</span></code> - member is not a function - MEDIUM</p></li>
<li><p><code class="docutils literal notranslate"><span class="pre">tarfile.extractall()</span></code> - members from the archive is trusted - HIGH</p></li>
</ul>
<p>Use <code class="docutils literal notranslate"><span class="pre">tarfile.extractall(members=function_name)</span></code> and define a function
that will inspect each member. Discard files that contain a directory
traversal sequences such as <code class="docutils literal notranslate"><span class="pre">../</span></code> or <code class="docutils literal notranslate"><span class="pre">\..</span></code> along with all special filetypes
unless you explicitly need them.</p>
<dl class="field-list simple">
<dt class="field-odd">Example<span class="colon">:</span></dt>
<dd class="field-odd"><p></p></dd>
</dl>
<div class="highlight-none notranslate"><div class="highlight"><pre><span></span>&gt;&gt; Issue: [B202:tarfile_unsafe_members] tarfile.extractall used without
any validation. You should check members and discard dangerous ones
Severity: High   Confidence: High
CWE: CWE-22 (https://cwe.mitre.org/data/definitions/22.html)
Location: examples/tarfile_extractall.py:8
More Info:
https://bandit.readthedocs.io/en/latest/plugins/b202_tarfile_unsafe_members.html
7       tar = tarfile.open(filename)
8       tar.extractall(path=tempfile.mkdtemp())
9       tar.close()
</pre></div>
</div>
<div class="admonition seealso">
<p class="admonition-title">See also</p>
<ul class="simple">
<li><p><a class="reference external" href="https://docs.python.org/3/library/tarfile.html#tarfile.TarFile.extractall">https://docs.python.org/3/library/tarfile.html#tarfile.TarFile.extractall</a></p></li>
<li><p><a class="reference external" href="https://docs.python.org/3/library/tarfile.html#tarfile.TarInfo">https://docs.python.org/3/library/tarfile.html#tarfile.TarInfo</a></p></li>
</ul>
</div>
<div class="versionadded">
<p><span class="versionmodified added">Added in version 1.7.5.</span></p>
</div>
<div class="versionchanged">
<p><span class="versionmodified changed">Changed in version 1.7.8: </span>Added check for filter parameter</p>
</div>
</section>

  <ul class="wy-breadcrumbs">
      <li><a href="../index.html" class="icon icon-home" aria-label="Home"></a></li>

          <div role="main" class="document" itemscope="itemscope" itemtype="http://schema.org/Article">
           <div itemprop="articleBody">
             
  <section id="b324-hashlib">
<h1>B324: hashlib<a class="headerlink" href="#b324-hashlib" title="Link to this heading"></a></h1>
<section id="b324-test-use-of-insecure-md4-md5-or-sha1-hash-functions-in-hashlib">
<h2>B324: Test use of insecure md4, md5, or sha1 hash functions in hashlib<a class="headerlink" href="#b324-test-use-of-insecure-md4-md5-or-sha1-hash-functions-in-hashlib" title="Link to this heading"></a></h2>
<p>This plugin checks for the usage of the insecure MD4, MD5, or SHA1 hash
functions in <code class="docutils literal notranslate"><span class="pre">hashlib</span></code> and <code class="docutils literal notranslate"><span class="pre">crypt</span></code>. The <code class="docutils literal notranslate"><span class="pre">hashlib.new</span></code> function provides
the ability to construct a new hashing object using the named algorithm. This
can be used to create insecure hash functions like MD4 and MD5 if they are
passed as algorithm names to this function.</p>
<p>This check does additional checking for usage of keyword usedforsecurity on all
function variations of hashlib.</p>
<p>Similar to <code class="docutils literal notranslate"><span class="pre">hashlib</span></code>, this plugin also checks for usage of one of the
<code class="docutils literal notranslate"><span class="pre">crypt</span></code> module’s weak hashes. <code class="docutils literal notranslate"><span class="pre">crypt</span></code> also permits MD5 among other weak
hash variants.</p>
<dl class="field-list simple">
<dt class="field-odd">Example<span class="colon">:</span></dt>
<dd class="field-odd"><p></p></dd>
</dl>
<div class="highlight-none notranslate"><div class="highlight"><pre><span></span>&gt;&gt; Issue: [B324:hashlib] Use of weak MD4, MD5, or SHA1 hash for
   security. Consider usedforsecurity=False
   Severity: High   Confidence: High
   CWE: CWE-327 (https://cwe.mitre.org/data/definitions/327.html)
   Location: examples/hashlib_new_insecure_functions.py:3:0
   More Info: https://bandit.readthedocs.io/en/latest/plugins/b324_hashlib.html
2
3   hashlib.new(&#39;md5&#39;)
4
</pre></div>
</div>
<div class="admonition seealso">
<p class="admonition-title">See also</p>
<ul class="simple">
<li><p><a class="reference external" href="https://cwe.mitre.org/data/definitions/327.html">https://cwe.mitre.org/data/definitions/327.html</a></p></li>
</ul>
</div>
<div class="versionadded">
<p><span class="versionmodified added">Added in version 1.5.0.</span></p>
</div>
<div class="versionchanged">
<p><span class="versionmodified changed">Changed in version 1.7.3: </span>CWE information added</p>
</div>
<div class="versionchanged">
<p><span class="versionmodified changed">Changed in version 1.7.6: </span>Added check for the crypt module weak hashes</p>
</div>
</section>
</section>

  <section id="b501-request-with-no-cert-validation">
<h1>B501: request_with_no_cert_validation<a class="headerlink" href="#b501-request-with-no-cert-validation" title="Link to this heading"></a></h1>
<section id="b501-test-for-missing-certificate-validation">
<h2>B501: Test for missing certificate validation<a class="headerlink" href="#b501-test-for-missing-certificate-validation" title="Link to this heading"></a></h2>
<p>Encryption in general is typically critical to the security of many
applications.  Using TLS can greatly increase security by guaranteeing the
identity of the party you are communicating with.  This is accomplished by one
or both parties presenting trusted certificates during the connection
initialization phase of TLS.</p>
<p>When HTTPS request methods are used, certificates are validated automatically
which is the desired behavior.  If certificate validation is explicitly turned
off Bandit will return a HIGH severity error.</p>
<dl class="field-list simple">
<dt class="field-odd">Example<span class="colon">:</span></dt>
<dd class="field-odd"><p></p></dd>
</dl>
<div class="highlight-none notranslate"><div class="highlight"><pre><span></span>&gt;&gt; Issue: [request_with_no_cert_validation] Call to requests with
verify=False disabling SSL certificate checks, security issue.
   Severity: High   Confidence: High
   CWE: CWE-295 (https://cwe.mitre.org/data/definitions/295.html)
   Location: examples/requests-ssl-verify-disabled.py:4
3   requests.get(&#39;https://gmail.com&#39;, verify=True)
4   requests.get(&#39;https://gmail.com&#39;, verify=False)
5   requests.post(&#39;https://gmail.com&#39;, verify=True)
</pre></div>
</div>
<div class="admonition seealso">
<p class="admonition-title">See also</p>
<ul class="simple">
<li><p><a class="reference external" href="https://security.openstack.org/guidelines/dg_move-data-securely.html">https://security.openstack.org/guidelines/dg_move-data-securely.html</a></p></li>
<li><p><a class="reference external" href="https://security.openstack.org/guidelines/dg_validate-certificates.html">https://security.openstack.org/guidelines/dg_validate-certificates.html</a></p></li>
<li><p><a class="reference external" href="https://cwe.mitre.org/data/definitions/295.html">https://cwe.mitre.org/data/definitions/295.html</a></p></li>
</ul>
</div>
<div class="versionadded">
<p><span class="versionmodified added">Added in version 0.9.0.</span></p>
</div>
<div class="versionchanged">
<p><span class="versionmodified changed">Changed in version 1.7.3: </span>CWE information added</p>
</div>
<div class="versionchanged">
<p><span class="versionmodified changed">Changed in version 1.7.5: </span>Added check for httpx module</p>
</div>
</section>

  <section id="b502-ssl-with-bad-version">
<h1>B502: ssl_with_bad_version<a class="headerlink" href="#b502-ssl-with-bad-version" title="Link to this heading"></a></h1>
<dl class="py function">
<dt class="sig sig-object py">
<span class="sig-prename descclassname"><span class="pre">bandit.plugins.insecure_ssl_tls.</span></span><span class="sig-name descname"><span class="pre">ssl_with_bad_version</span></span><span class="sig-paren">(</span><em class="sig-param"><span class="n"><span class="pre">context</span></span></em>, <em class="sig-param"><span class="n"><span class="pre">config</span></span></em><span class="sig-paren">)</span><a class="reference internal" href="../_modules/bandit/plugins/insecure_ssl_tls.html#ssl_with_bad_version"><span class="viewcode-link"><span class="pre">[source]</span></span></a></dt>
<dd><p><strong>B502: Test for SSL use with bad version used</strong></p>
<p>Several highly publicized exploitable flaws have been discovered
in all versions of SSL and early versions of TLS. It is strongly
recommended that use of the following known broken protocol versions be
avoided:</p>
<ul class="simple">
<li><p>SSL v2</p></li>
<li><p>SSL v3</p></li>
<li><p>TLS v1</p></li>
<li><p>TLS v1.1</p></li>
</ul>
<p>This plugin test scans for calls to Python methods with parameters that
indicate the used broken SSL/TLS protocol versions. Currently, detection
supports methods using Python’s native SSL/TLS support and the pyOpenSSL
module. A HIGH severity warning will be reported whenever known broken
protocol versions are detected.</p>
<p>It is worth noting that native support for TLS 1.2 is only available in
more recent Python versions, specifically 2.7.9 and up, and 3.x</p>
<p>A note on ‘SSLv23’:</p>
<p>Amongst the available SSL/TLS versions provided by Python/pyOpenSSL there
exists the option to use SSLv23. This very poorly named option actually
means “use the highest version of SSL/TLS supported by both the server and
client”. This may (and should be) a version well in advance of SSL v2 or
v3. Bandit can scan for the use of SSLv23 if desired, but its detection
does not necessarily indicate a problem.</p>
<p>When using SSLv23 it is important to also provide flags to explicitly
exclude bad versions of SSL/TLS from the protocol versions considered. Both
the Python native and pyOpenSSL modules provide the <code class="docutils literal notranslate"><span class="pre">OP_NO_SSLv2</span></code> and
<code class="docutils literal notranslate"><span class="pre">OP_NO_SSLv3</span></code> flags for this purpose.</p>
<p><strong>Config Options:</strong></p>
<div class="highlight-yaml notranslate"><div class="highlight"><pre><span></span><span class="nt">ssl_with_bad_version</span><span class="p">:</span>
<span class="w">    </span><span class="nt">bad_protocol_versions</span><span class="p">:</span>
<span class="w">        </span><span class="p p-Indicator">-</span><span class="w"> </span><span class="l l-Scalar l-Scalar-Plain">PROTOCOL_SSLv2</span>
<span class="w">        </span><span class="p p-Indicator">-</span><span class="w"> </span><span class="l l-Scalar l-Scalar-Plain">SSLv2_METHOD</span>
<span class="w">        </span><span class="p p-Indicator">-</span><span class="w"> </span><span class="l l-Scalar l-Scalar-Plain">SSLv23_METHOD</span>
<span class="w">        </span><span class="p p-Indicator">-</span><span class="w"> </span><span class="l l-Scalar l-Scalar-Plain">PROTOCOL_SSLv3</span><span class="w">  </span><span class="c1"># strict option</span>
<span class="w">        </span><span class="p p-Indicator">-</span><span class="w"> </span><span class="l l-Scalar l-Scalar-Plain">PROTOCOL_TLSv1</span><span class="w">  </span><span class="c1"># strict option</span>
<span class="w">        </span><span class="p p-Indicator">-</span><span class="w"> </span><span class="l l-Scalar l-Scalar-Plain">SSLv3_METHOD</span><span class="w">    </span><span class="c1"># strict option</span>
<span class="w">        </span><span class="p p-Indicator">-</span><span class="w"> </span><span class="l l-Scalar l-Scalar-Plain">TLSv1_METHOD</span><span class="w">    </span><span class="c1"># strict option</span>
</pre></div>
</div>
<dl class="field-list simple">
<dt class="field-odd">Example<span class="colon">:</span></dt>
<dd class="field-odd"><p></p></dd>
</dl>
<div class="highlight-none notranslate"><div class="highlight"><pre><span></span>&gt;&gt; Issue: ssl.wrap_socket call with insecure SSL/TLS protocol version
identified, security issue.
   Severity: High   Confidence: High
   CWE: CWE-327 (https://cwe.mitre.org/data/definitions/327.html)
   Location: ./examples/ssl-insecure-version.py:13
12  # strict tests
13  ssl.wrap_socket(ssl_version=ssl.PROTOCOL_SSLv3)
14  ssl.wrap_socket(ssl_version=ssl.PROTOCOL_TLSv1)
</pre></div>
</div>
<div class="admonition seealso">
<p class="admonition-title">See also</p>
<ul class="simple">
<li><p><code class="xref py py-func docutils literal notranslate"><span class="pre">ssl_with_bad_defaults()</span></code></p></li>
<li><p><code class="xref py py-func docutils literal notranslate"><span class="pre">ssl_with_no_version()</span></code></p></li>
<li><p><a class="reference external" href="https://heartbleed.com/">https://heartbleed.com/</a></p></li>
<li><p><a class="reference external" href="https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/POODLE">https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/POODLE</a></p></li>
<li><p><a class="reference external" href="https://security.openstack.org/guidelines/dg_move-data-securely.html">https://security.openstack.org/guidelines/dg_move-data-securely.html</a></p></li>
<li><p><a class="reference external" href="https://cwe.mitre.org/data/definitions/327.html">https://cwe.mitre.org/data/definitions/327.html</a></p></li>
</ul>
</div>
<div class="versionadded">
<p><span class="versionmodified added">Added in version 0.9.0.</span></p>
</div>
<div class="versionchanged">
<p><span class="versionmodified changed">Changed in version 1.7.3: </span>CWE information added</p>
</div>
<div class="versionchanged">
<p><span class="versionmodified changed">Changed in version 1.7.5: </span>Added TLS 1.1</p>
</div>
</dd></dl>

  <section id="b503-ssl-with-bad-defaults">
<h1>B503: ssl_with_bad_defaults<a class="headerlink" href="#b503-ssl-with-bad-defaults" title="Link to this heading"></a></h1>
<dl class="py function">
<dt class="sig sig-object py">
<span class="sig-prename descclassname"><span class="pre">bandit.plugins.insecure_ssl_tls.</span></span><span class="sig-name descname"><span class="pre">ssl_with_bad_defaults</span></span><span class="sig-paren">(</span><em class="sig-param"><span class="n"><span class="pre">context</span></span></em>, <em class="sig-param"><span class="n"><span class="pre">config</span></span></em><span class="sig-paren">)</span><a class="reference internal" href="../_modules/bandit/plugins/insecure_ssl_tls.html#ssl_with_bad_defaults"><span class="viewcode-link"><span class="pre">[source]</span></span></a></dt>
<dd><p><strong>B503: Test for SSL use with bad defaults specified</strong></p>
<p>This plugin is part of a family of tests that detect the use of known bad
versions of SSL/TLS, please see <span class="xref std std-doc">../plugins/ssl_with_bad_version</span> for
a complete discussion. Specifically, this plugin test scans for Python
methods with default parameter values that specify the use of broken
SSL/TLS protocol versions. Currently, detection supports methods using
Python’s native SSL/TLS support and the pyOpenSSL module. A MEDIUM severity
warning will be reported whenever known broken protocol versions are
detected.</p>
<p><strong>Config Options:</strong></p>
<p>This test shares the configuration provided for the standard
<span class="xref std std-doc">../plugins/ssl_with_bad_version</span> test, please refer to its
documentation.</p>
<dl class="field-list simple">
<dt class="field-odd">Example<span class="colon">:</span></dt>
<dd class="field-odd"><p></p></dd>
</dl>
<div class="highlight-none notranslate"><div class="highlight"><pre><span></span>&gt;&gt; Issue: Function definition identified with insecure SSL/TLS protocol
version by default, possible security issue.
   Severity: Medium   Confidence: Medium
   CWE: CWE-327 (https://cwe.mitre.org/data/definitions/327.html)
   Location: ./examples/ssl-insecure-version.py:28
27
28  def open_ssl_socket(version=SSL.SSLv2_METHOD):
29      pass
</pre></div>
</div>
<div class="admonition seealso">
<p class="admonition-title">See also</p>
<ul class="simple">
<li><p><code class="xref py py-func docutils literal notranslate"><span class="pre">ssl_with_bad_version()</span></code></p></li>
<li><p><code class="xref py py-func docutils literal notranslate"><span class="pre">ssl_with_no_version()</span></code></p></li>
<li><p><a class="reference external" href="https://heartbleed.com/">https://heartbleed.com/</a></p></li>
<li><p><a class="reference external" href="https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/POODLE">https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/POODLE</a></p></li>
<li><p><a class="reference external" href="https://security.openstack.org/guidelines/dg_move-data-securely.html">https://security.openstack.org/guidelines/dg_move-data-securely.html</a></p></li>
</ul>
</div>
<div class="versionadded">
<p><span class="versionmodified added">Added in version 0.9.0.</span></p>
</div>
<div class="versionchanged">

</div>
<div class="versionchanged">
<p><span class="versionmodified changed">Changed in version 1.7.5: </span>Added TLS 1.1</p>
</div>
</dd></dl>

          <div role="main" class="document" itemscope="itemscope" itemtype="http://schema.org/Article">
           <div itemprop="articleBody">
             
  <section id="b504-ssl-with-no-version">
<h1>B504: ssl_with_no_version<a class="headerlink" href="#b504-ssl-with-no-version" title="Link to this heading"></a></h1>
<dl class="py function">
<dt class="sig sig-object py">
<span class="sig-prename descclassname"><span class="pre">bandit.plugins.insecure_ssl_tls.</span></span><span class="sig-name descname"><span class="pre">ssl_with_no_version</span></span><span class="sig-paren">(</span><em class="sig-param"><span class="n"><span class="pre">context</span></span></em><span class="sig-paren">)</span><a class="reference internal" href="../_modules/bandit/plugins/insecure_ssl_tls.html#ssl_with_no_version"><span class="viewcode-link"><span class="pre">[source]</span></span></a></dt>
<dd><p><strong>B504: Test for SSL use with no version specified</strong></p>
<p>This plugin is part of a family of tests that detect the use of known bad
versions of SSL/TLS, please see <span class="xref std std-doc">../plugins/ssl_with_bad_version</span> for
a complete discussion. Specifically, This plugin test scans for specific
methods in Python’s native SSL/TLS support and the pyOpenSSL module that
configure the version of SSL/TLS protocol to use. These methods are known
to provide default value that maximize compatibility, but permit use of the
aforementioned broken protocol versions. A LOW severity warning will be
reported whenever this is detected.</p>
<p><strong>Config Options:</strong></p>
<p>This test shares the configuration provided for the standard
<span class="xref std std-doc">../plugins/ssl_with_bad_version</span> test, please refer to its
documentation.</p>
<dl class="field-list simple">
<dt class="field-odd">Example<span class="colon">:</span></dt>
<dd class="field-odd"><p></p></dd>
</dl>
<div class="highlight-none notranslate"><div class="highlight"><pre><span></span>&gt;&gt; Issue: ssl.wrap_socket call with no SSL/TLS protocol version
specified, the default SSLv23 could be insecure, possible security
issue.
   Severity: Low   Confidence: Medium
   CWE: CWE-327 (https://cwe.mitre.org/data/definitions/327.html)
   Location: ./examples/ssl-insecure-version.py:23
22
23  ssl.wrap_socket()
24
</pre></div>
</div>
<div class="admonition seealso">
<p class="admonition-title">See also</p>
<ul class="simple">
<li><p><code class="xref py py-func docutils literal notranslate"><span class="pre">ssl_with_bad_version()</span></code></p></li>
<li><p><code class="xref py py-func docutils literal notranslate"><span class="pre">ssl_with_bad_defaults()</span></code></p></li>
<li><p><a class="reference external" href="https://heartbleed.com/">https://heartbleed.com/</a></p></li>
<li><p><a class="reference external" href="https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/POODLE">https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/POODLE</a></p></li>
<li><p><a class="reference external" href="https://security.openstack.org/guidelines/dg_move-data-securely.html">https://security.openstack.org/guidelines/dg_move-data-securely.html</a></p></li>
</ul>
</div>
<div class="versionadded">
<p><span class="versionmodified added">Added in version 0.9.0.</span></p>
</div>
<div class="versionchanged">
<p><span class="versionmodified changed">Changed in version 1.7.3: </span>CWE information added</p>
</div>
</dd></dl>

  <section id="b505-weak-cryptographic-key">
<h1>B505: weak_cryptographic_key<a class="headerlink" href="#b505-weak-cryptographic-key" title="Link to this heading"></a></h1>
<section id="b505-test-for-weak-cryptographic-key-use">
<h2>B505: Test for weak cryptographic key use<a class="headerlink" href="#b505-test-for-weak-cryptographic-key-use" title="Link to this heading"></a></h2>
<p>As computational power increases, so does the ability to break ciphers with
smaller key lengths. The recommended key length size for RSA and DSA algorithms
is 2048 and higher. 1024 bits and below are now considered breakable. EC key
length sizes are recommended to be 224 and higher with 160 and below considered
breakable. This plugin test checks for use of any key less than those limits
and returns a high severity error if lower than the lower threshold and a
medium severity error for those lower than the higher threshold.</p>
<dl class="field-list simple">
<dt class="field-odd">Example<span class="colon">:</span></dt>
<dd class="field-odd"><p></p></dd>
</dl>
<div class="highlight-none notranslate"><div class="highlight"><pre><span></span>&gt;&gt; Issue: DSA key sizes below 1024 bits are considered breakable.
   Severity: High   Confidence: High
   CWE: CWE-326 (https://cwe.mitre.org/data/definitions/326.html)
   Location: examples/weak_cryptographic_key_sizes.py:36
35  # Also incorrect: without keyword args
36  dsa.generate_private_key(512,
37                           backends.default_backend())
38  rsa.generate_private_key(3,
</pre></div>
</div>
<div class="admonition seealso">
<p class="admonition-title">See also</p>
<ul class="simple">
<li><p><a class="reference external" href="https://csrc.nist.gov/publications/detail/sp/800-131a/rev-2/final">https://csrc.nist.gov/publications/detail/sp/800-131a/rev-2/final</a></p></li>
<li><p><a class="reference external" href="https://security.openstack.org/guidelines/dg_strong-crypto.html">https://security.openstack.org/guidelines/dg_strong-crypto.html</a></p></li>
<li><p><a class="reference external" href="https://cwe.mitre.org/data/definitions/326.html">https://cwe.mitre.org/data/definitions/326.html</a></p></li>
</ul>
</div>

<p><span class="versionmodified added">Added in version 0.14.0.</span></p>
</div>

</div>

  <section id="b506-yaml-load">
<h1>B506: yaml_load<a class="headerlink" href="#b506-yaml-load" title="Link to this heading"></a></h1>
<section id="b506-test-for-use-of-yaml-load">
<h2>B506: Test for use of yaml load<a class="headerlink" href="#b506-test-for-use-of-yaml-load" title="Link to this heading"></a></h2>
<p>This plugin test checks for the unsafe usage of the <code class="docutils literal notranslate"><span class="pre">yaml.load</span></code> function from
the PyYAML package. The yaml.load function provides the ability to construct
an arbitrary Python object, which may be dangerous if you receive a YAML
document from an untrusted source. The function yaml.safe_load limits this
ability to simple Python objects like integers or lists.</p>
<p>Please see
<a class="reference external" href="https://pyyaml.org/wiki/PyYAMLDocumentation#LoadingYAML">https://pyyaml.org/wiki/PyYAMLDocumentation#LoadingYAML</a> for more information
on <code class="docutils literal notranslate"><span class="pre">yaml.load</span></code> and yaml.safe_load</p>
<dl class="field-list simple">
<dt class="field-odd">Example<span class="colon">:</span></dt>
<dd class="field-odd"><p></p></dd>
</dl>
<div class="highlight-none notranslate"><div class="highlight"><pre><span></span>&gt;&gt; Issue: [yaml_load] Use of unsafe yaml load. Allows instantiation of
   arbitrary objects. Consider yaml.safe_load().
   Severity: Medium   Confidence: High
   CWE: CWE-20 (https://cwe.mitre.org/data/definitions/20.html)
   Location: examples/yaml_load.py:5
4 ystr = yaml.dump({&#39;a&#39; : 1, &#39;b&#39; : 2, &#39;c&#39; : 3})
5 y = yaml.load(ystr)
6 yaml.dump(y)
</pre></div>
</div>
<div class="admonition seealso">
<p class="admonition-title">See also</p>
<ul class="simple">
<li><p><a class="reference external" href="https://pyyaml.org/wiki/PyYAMLDocumentation#LoadingYAML">https://pyyaml.org/wiki/PyYAMLDocumentation#LoadingYAML</a></p></li>
<li><p><a class="reference external" href="https://cwe.mitre.org/data/definitions/20.html">https://cwe.mitre.org/data/definitions/20.html</a></p></li>
</ul>
</div>
<div class="versionadded">
<p><span class="versionmodified added">Added in version 1.0.0.</span></p>
</div>
<div class="versionchanged">
<p><span class="versionmodified changed">Changed in version 1.7.3: </span>CWE information added</p>
</div>
</section>
</section>

  <section id="b507-ssh-no-host-key-verification">
<h1>B507: ssh_no_host_key_verification<a class="headerlink" href="#b507-ssh-no-host-key-verification" title="Link to this heading"></a></h1>
<section id="b507-test-for-missing-host-key-validation">
<h2>B507: Test for missing host key validation<a class="headerlink" href="#b507-test-for-missing-host-key-validation" title="Link to this heading"></a></h2>
<p>Encryption in general is typically critical to the security of many
applications.  Using SSH can greatly increase security by guaranteeing the
identity of the party you are communicating with.  This is accomplished by one
or both parties presenting trusted host keys during the connection
initialization phase of SSH.</p>
<p>When paramiko methods are used, host keys are verified by default. If host key
verification is disabled, Bandit will return a HIGH severity error.</p>
<dl class="field-list simple">
<dt class="field-odd">Example<span class="colon">:</span></dt>
<dd class="field-odd"><p></p></dd>
</dl>
<div class="highlight-none notranslate"><div class="highlight"><pre><span></span>&gt;&gt; Issue: [B507:ssh_no_host_key_verification] Paramiko call with policy set
to automatically trust the unknown host key.
Severity: High   Confidence: Medium
CWE: CWE-295 (https://cwe.mitre.org/data/definitions/295.html)
Location: examples/no_host_key_verification.py:4
3   ssh_client = client.SSHClient()
4   ssh_client.set_missing_host_key_policy(client.AutoAddPolicy)
5   ssh_client.set_missing_host_key_policy(client.WarningPolicy)
</pre></div>
</div>
<div class="versionadded">
<p><span class="versionmodified added">Added in version 1.5.1.</span></p>
</div>
<div class="versionchanged">
<p><span class="versionmodified changed">Changed in version 1.7.3: </span>CWE information added</p>
</div>
</section>
</section>
